    "Title": "ProtoDiff: Learning to Learn Prototypical Networks by Task-Guided Diffusion",
    "Main Contributions": "The paper addresses the fragility of estimating deterministic prototypes from a limited number of examples in few-shot learning, which often leads to biased class representations. It introduces ProtoDiff, a novel framework that leverages a task-guided diffusion model during meta-training to gradually generate efficient, task-specific class representations (prototypes). Key contributions include: 1) Optimizing a set of prototypes for per-task overfitting to serve as ground truth representations. 2) Implementing a task-guided diffusion process within the prototype space to meta-learn a generative process from vanilla to overfitted prototypes. 3) Generating task-specific prototypes from random noise during meta-test conditioned on limited samples. 4) Proposing residual prototype learning to accelerate training and enhance performance. ProtoDiff achieves new state-of-the-art performance across within-domain, cross-domain, and few-task few-shot classification, demonstrating its ability to capture underlying prototype distributions and improve generalization.",
    "Methodology": "ProtoDiff builds upon prototypical networks and diffusion models. Its methodology involves three main steps: 1) Per-task prototype overfitting: A meta-learner is initially trained on a complete set of tasks and then fine-tuned on each individual task to obtain 'overfitted prototypes' (zi,∗), which are considered optimal ground truth representations. 2) Task-guided diffusion: During meta-training, a transformer-based diffusion model (zθ) learns to predict the denoised overfitted prototype. It takes a noised overfitted prototype, a 'vanilla prototype' (˜z, computed as a simple average from the support set), and a time step as input. The objective minimizes a simplified variational lower bound combined with cross-entropy loss. During meta-test, the model generates task-specific prototypes by starting from Gaussian noise and gradually denoising it, conditioned on the vanilla prototype derived from the new task's support set. The diffusion model uses a transformer architecture inspired by GPT-2. 3) Residual prototype learning: Instead of directly predicting the overfitted prototype z∗, the model predicts the residual update (z∗ − ˜z), leveraging its sparsity to accelerate training and enable identity initialization.",
    "Experimental Setup": "Experiments evaluate ProtoDiff across three few-shot learning scenarios: within-domain, cross-domain, and few-task. For within-domain, datasets used are miniImagenet, tieredImagenet, and ImageNet-800, with Conv-4, ResNet-12, and ResNet-50 backbones. Cross-domain experiments train on miniImagenet and test on CropDisease, EuroSAT, ISIC2018, and ChestX, using a ResNet-10 backbone. Few-task few-shot learning is tested on miniImagenet-S, ISIC, DermNet-S, and Tabular Murris, employing a network with four convolutional blocks. Evaluation uses average top-1 classification accuracy with 95% confidence intervals across various N-way K-shot settings. ProtoDiff is compared against numerous state-of-the-art prototype-based meta-learning models and other generative models (VAE, Normalizing Flows). Optimization uses SGD, with specific learning rate schedules and data augmentation. Experiments were performed on a single A100 GPU.",
    "Limitations": "The diffusion model in ProtoDiff requires a substantial number of timesteps for sampling during the meta-test stage, leading to higher computational resource usage compared to vanilla prototypes, even with DDIM sampling. Additionally, obtaining the overfitted prototype involves a fine-tuning step, which increases the overall training time. Specifically, ProtoDiff is slower by factors of 5x during meta-training and 15x during meta-testing in terms of wall-clock times per task compared to ProtoNet.",
    "Future Research Directions": "Future work will focus on investigating and addressing the identified computational limitations. This includes reducing the number of timesteps required for diffusion sampling and optimizing the fine-tuning process for obtaining overfitted prototypes to further enhance the applicability and efficiency of the ProtoDiff approach.",
    "Experiment Code": "File Path: overfit/overfiting.py\nContent:\nimport numpy as np\nimport torch\nimport torch.nn.functional as F\nimport copy\nimport utils\n\ndef train_overfitting(bmodel, x_shot, x_query, label, n_train_way=5, lr=0.001, n_iteration=5):\n    batch_prototype = []\n\n    shot_shape = x_shot.shape[:-3]\n    query_shape = x_query.shape[:-3]\n    img_shape = x_shot.shape[-3:]\n    x_query = x_query.view(-1, *img_shape)\n    x_shot_ori = x_shot.view(-1, *img_shape)\n    x_query = bmodel.encoder(x_query)\n    x_query = x_query.view(*query_shape, -1)\n    x_shot_ori = bmodel.encoder(x_shot_ori)\n    x_shot_ori = x_shot_ori.view(*shot_shape, -1)\n    x_query_feature = x_query.detach()\n    x_query_norm = F.normalize(x_query_feature, dim=-1)\n\n\n    for i in range(x_shot.shape[0]):\n        base_model = copy.deepcopy(bmodel)\n        param_weight = base_model.parameters()\n        opt = torch.optim.Adam(\n            param_weight, lr=lr)\n        batch_shot = torch.unsqueeze(x_shot[i], dim=0)\n        batch_label = label[i * int(label.shape[0] / x_shot.shape[0]):(i + 1) * int(label.shape[0] / x_shot.shape[0])]\n        shot_shape = batch_shot[0].shape[:-3]\n\n        for epoch in range(n_iteration):\n            opt.zero_grad()\n            batch_x_shot = batch_shot.view(-1, *img_shape)\n            base_support_features = base_model.encoder(batch_x_shot)\n            base_support_features = base_support_features.view(*shot_shape, -1)\n            base_support_features = base_support_features.mean(dim=-2)\n            x_shot_prototype_norm = F.normalize(base_support_features, dim=-1)\n\n            predicted_labels = utils.compute_logits(\n                x_query_norm[i], x_shot_prototype_norm, metric='dot')\n\n            predicted_labels = predicted_labels.view(-1, n_train_way)\n            loss = F.cross_entropy(predicted_labels, batch_label)\n            # acc = utils.compute_acc(predicted_labels, batch_label)\n            # print(\"epoch:\" + str(epoch) + \", acc:\" + str(acc) + \", loss:\"+str(loss.item()))\n            loss.backward()\n            opt.step()\n\n        batch_prototype.append(base_support_features)\n        for param in base_model.parameters():\n            param.detach()\n    del base_model\n    batch_prototype = torch.stack(batch_prototype).detach()\n\n    return batch_prototype, x_shot_ori\n\nFile Path: models/transformer.py\nContent:\n\"\"\"\nThis file contains the G.pt model and its building blocks (minGPT without masking, etc.).\n\"\"\"\nimport math\n\nimport numpy as np\nimport torch\nimport torch.nn as nn\n\nfrom copy import deepcopy\nfrom torch.nn import functional as F\n\n\nclass SelfAttention(nn.Module):\n    \"\"\"\n    A vanilla multi-head self-attention layer with a projection at the end.\n    It is possible to use torch.nn.MultiheadAttention here but I am including an\n    explicit implementation here to show that there is nothing too scary here.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, n_embd, n_head, attn_pdrop=0.0, resid_pdrop=0.0):\n        super().__init__()\n        assert n_embd % n_head == 0\n        # key, query, value projections for all heads\n        self.key = nn.Linear(n_embd, n_embd)\n        self.query = nn.Linear(n_embd, n_embd)\n        self.value = nn.Linear(n_embd, n_embd)\n        # regularization\n        self.attn_drop = nn.Dropout(attn_pdrop)\n        self.resid_drop = nn.Dropout(resid_pdrop)\n        # output projection\n        self.proj = nn.Linear(n_embd, n_embd)\n\n        self.n_head = n_head\n\n    def forward(self, x):\n        B, T, C = x.size()\n\n        # calculate query, key, values for all heads in batch and move head forward to be the batch dim\n        k = self.key(x).view(B, T, self.n_head, C // self.n_head).transpose(1, 2)  # (B, nh, T, hs)\n        q = self.query(x).view(B, T, self.n_head, C // self.n_head).transpose(1, 2)  # (B, nh, T, hs)\n        v = self.value(x).view(B, T, self.n_head, C // self.n_head).transpose(1, 2)  # (B, nh, T, hs)\n\n        # self-attention; Self-attend: (B, nh, T, hs) x (B, nh, hs, T) -> (B, nh, T, T)\n        att = (q @ k.transpose(-2, -1)) * (1.0 / math.sqrt(k.size(-1)))\n        att = F.softmax(att, dim=-1)\n        att = self.attn_drop(att)\n        y = att @ v  # (B, nh, T, T) x (B, nh, T, hs) -> (B, nh, T, hs)\n        y = y.transpose(1, 2).contiguous().view(B, T, C)  # re-assemble all head outputs side by side\n\n        # output projection\n        y = self.resid_drop(self.proj(y))\n        return y\n\n\nclass Block(nn.Module):\n    \"\"\" an unassuming Transformer block \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, n_embd, n_head, attn_pdrop=0.0, resid_pdrop=0.0):\n        super().__init__()\n        self.ln1 = nn.LayerNorm(n_embd)\n        self.ln2 = nn.LayerNorm(n_embd)\n        self.attn = SelfAttention(n_embd, n_head, attn_pdrop, resid_pdrop)\n        self.mlp = nn.Sequential(\n            nn.Linear(n_embd, 4 * n_embd),\n            nn.GELU(),\n            nn.Linear(4 * n_embd, n_embd),\n            nn.Dropout(resid_pdrop),\n        )\n\n    def forward(self, x):\n        x = x + self.attn(self.ln1(x))\n        x = x + self.mlp(self.ln2(x))\n        return x\n\n\nclass GPT(nn.Module):\n    \"\"\"\n    Modified from: https://github.com/karpathy/minGPT\n    A version of minGPT without masking (we use diffusion instead).\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(\n        self, input_prototype_sizes, len_input,\n        n_layer=12, n_head=12, n_embd=512, encoder_depth=1, attn_pdrop=0.0,\n        resid_pdrop=0.0, embd_pdrop=0.0,\n    ):\n        super().__init__()\n        # Determine how many parameters are placed into each individual Transformer token:\n        self.input_splits = input_prototype_sizes\n        block_size = len_input\n\n\n        # input embedding stem\n        self.pos_emb = nn.Parameter(torch.zeros(1, block_size, n_embd))\n        self.drop = nn.Dropout(embd_pdrop)\n        # transformer\n        self.blocks = nn.Sequential(\n            *[Block(n_embd, n_head, attn_pdrop, resid_pdrop) for _ in range(n_layer)]\n        )\n\n        self.block_size = block_size\n        self.input_prototype_projections = self.build_encoder(n_embd, encoder_depth, self.input_splits)\n        self.ln_in = nn.LayerNorm(n_embd)\n        self.apply(self._init_weights)\n\n    @staticmethod\n    def build_encoder(n_embd, encoder_depth, input_splits):\n        input_prototype_projections = nn.ModuleList()\n        for len_input in input_splits:\n            in_proj = [nn.Linear(len_input, n_embd, bias=False)]\n            for _ in range(encoder_depth - 1):\n                in_proj.append(nn.GELU())\n                in_proj.append(nn.Linear(n_embd, n_embd, bias=False))\n            in_proj = nn.Sequential(*in_proj)\n            input_prototype_projections.append(in_proj)\n        return input_prototype_projections\n\n    @staticmethod\n    def build_decoder(n_embd, decoder_depth, output_splits):\n        # Create a unique MLP decoder for each noised token\n        output_parameter_projections = nn.ModuleList()\n        for output_chunk_size in output_splits:\n            out_proj = []\n            for _ in range(decoder_depth - 1):\n                out_proj.append(nn.Linear(n_embd, n_embd, bias=False))\n                out_proj.append(nn.GELU())\n            out_proj.append(nn.Linear(n_embd, output_chunk_size, bias=False))\n            out_proj = nn.Sequential(*out_proj)\n            output_parameter_projections.append(out_proj)\n        return output_parameter_projections\n\n    def get_block_size(self):\n        return self.block_size\n\n    @staticmethod\n    def _init_weights(module):\n        if isinstance(module, (nn.Linear, nn.Conv1d, nn.Embedding)):\n            module.weight.data.normal_(mean=0.0, std=0.02)\n            if isinstance(module, (nn.Linear, nn.Conv1d)) and module.bias is not None:\n                module.bias.data.zero_()\n        elif isinstance(module, nn.LayerNorm):\n            module.bias.data.zero_()\n            module.weight.data.fill_(1.0)\n    def encode_prototypes(self, parameters):\n        \"\"\"\n        Chunk input parameter vector, apply per-chunk encoding, and\n        stack projected chunks along the sequence (token) dimension.\n        \"\"\"\n        assert len(parameters) == 3\n        representations = []\n        for parameter, in_proj in zip(parameters, self.input_prototype_projections):\n            representations.append(in_proj(parameter))\n        representations = torch.cat(representations, dim=1)  # (b, t, d)\n        representations = self.ln_in(representations)\n        assert representations.dim() == 3\n        return representations\n\n    def forward(self, x):\n        embeddings = self.encode_prototypes(x)\n        b, t, d = embeddings.size()\n        assert t == self.block_size, f\"Expected {self.block_size} tokens on dim=1, but got {t}\"\n        # forward the GPT model\n        position_embeddings = self.pos_emb[:, :t, :]  # each position maps to a (learnable) vector\n        x = self.drop(embeddings + position_embeddings)\n        x = self.blocks(x)\n\n        return x\n\n\nclass FrequencyEmbedder(nn.Module):\n\n    def __init__(self, num_frequencies, max_freq_log2):\n        super().__init__()\n        frequencies = 2 ** torch.linspace(0, max_freq_log2, steps=num_frequencies)\n        self.register_buffer('frequencies', frequencies)\n\n    def forward(self, x):\n        # x should be of size (N,) or (N, D)\n        N = x.size(0)\n        if x.dim() == 1:  # (N,)\n            x = x.unsqueeze(1)  # (N, D) where D=1\n        x_unsqueezed = x.unsqueeze(-1).to('cuda', torch.float)  # (N, D, 1)\n        scaled = self.frequencies.view(1, 1, -1) * x_unsqueezed  # (N, D, num_frequencies)\n        s = torch.sin(scaled)\n        c = torch.cos(scaled)\n        #embedded = torch.cat([s, c], dim=-1).view(N, -1)\n        embedded = torch.cat([s, c, x_unsqueezed], dim=-1).view(N, -1)\n        return embedded\n\n\nclass Gpt(nn.Module):\n\n    \"\"\"\n    The G.pt model.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(\n        self, \n        prototype_sizes=512,                    # A list of integers indicating the total number of parameters in each layer\n        num_frequencies=256,                # number of frequencies sampled for embedding scalars\n        max_freq_log2=20,                   # max log2 frequency for embedding scalars\n        predict_xstart=True,                # if True, G.pt predicts signal (False = predict noise)\n        use_global_residual=True,\n        **gpt_kwargs                        # Arguments for the Transformer model (depth, heads, etc.)\n    ):\n        super().__init__()\n        self.predict_xstart = predict_xstart\n        input_prototype_sizes = self.compute_token_sizes(prototype_sizes, num_frequencies)\n\n        self.decoder = GPT(input_prototype_sizes, **gpt_kwargs)\n        self.scalar_embedder = FrequencyEmbedder(num_frequencies, max_freq_log2)\n\n        # Initialize with identity output:\n        self.use_global_residual = use_global_residual\n\n    @staticmethod\n    def get_scalar_token_size(num_frequencies):\n        \"\"\"\n        Computes the size of each metadata token after being projected by the frequency embedder.\n        \"\"\"\n        return num_frequencies * 2 + 1\n\n    def compute_token_sizes(self, parameter_sizes, num_frequencies):\n        input_parameter_sizes = [deepcopy(parameter_sizes)]\n        # account for the second weight vector that will be input:\n        input_parameter_sizes.append(input_parameter_sizes[0])\n        # Account for the scalar inputs (diffusion timestep and loss/error/return inputs):\n        scalar_token_size = [self.get_scalar_token_size(num_frequencies)]\n        input_parameter_sizes.extend(scalar_token_size)\n        return input_parameter_sizes\n\n    @torch.no_grad()\n    def gradient_norm(self):\n        \"\"\"\n        Computes the gradient norm for monitoring purposes.\n        \"\"\"\n        total_norm = 0.0\n        for p in self.parameters():\n            param_norm = p.grad.detach().data.norm(2)\n            total_norm += param_norm.item() ** 2\n        total_norm = total_norm ** 0.5\n        return total_norm\n\n\n    def forward(self, x, x_ori, t):\n        x_prototype = torch.mean(x_ori, dim=1)\n        x_diff = x - x_prototype\n        normalize = x_diff.view(x_diff.shape[0], -1).std(-1).unsqueeze(1)\n        x_norm = x_diff / normalize\n        t_embedding = self.scalar_embedder(t)\n        inp = [torch.unsqueeze(x_norm, dim=1), x_ori, torch.unsqueeze(t_embedding, dim=1)]\n        output = self.decoder(inp)\n\n        return output\n\nFile Path: diffusion/gaussian_diffusion.py\nContent:\n\"\"\"\nModified from OpenAI's diffusion repos\n    GLIDE: https://github.com/openai/glide-text2im/blob/main/glide_text2im/gaussian_diffusion.py\n    ADM:   https://github.com/openai/guided-diffusion/blob/main/guided_diffusion\n    IDDPM: https://github.com/openai/improved-diffusion/blob/main/improved_diffusion/gaussian_diffusion.py\n\"\"\"\n\nimport math\n\nimport numpy as np\nimport torch as th\nimport enum\n\nfrom .diffusion_utils import mean_flat, normal_kl, continuous_gaussian_log_likelihood\n\n\nclass ModelMeanType(enum.Enum):\n    \"\"\"\n    Which type of output the model predicts.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    PREVIOUS_X = enum.auto()  # the model predicts x_{t-1}\n    START_X = enum.auto()  # the model predicts x_0\n    EPSILON = enum.auto()  # the model predicts epsilon\n\n\nclass ModelVarType(enum.Enum):\n    \"\"\"\n    What is used as the model's output variance.\n    The LEARNED_RANGE option has been added to allow the model to predict\n    values between FIXED_SMALL and FIXED_LARGE, making its job easier.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    LEARNED = enum.auto()\n    FIXED_SMALL = enum.auto()\n    FIXED_LARGE = enum.auto()\n    LEARNED_RANGE = enum.auto()\n\n\nclass LossType(enum.Enum):\n    MSE = enum.auto()  # use raw MSE loss (and KL when learning variances)\n    RESCALED_MSE = (\n        enum.auto()\n    )  # use raw MSE loss (with RESCALED_KL when learning variances)\n    KL = enum.auto()  # use the variational lower-bound\n    RESCALED_KL = enum.auto()  # like KL, but rescale to estimate the full VLB\n\n    def is_vb(self):\n        return self == LossType.KL or self == LossType.RESCALED_KL\n\n\ndef _warmup_beta(beta_start, beta_end, num_diffusion_timesteps, warmup_frac):\n    betas = beta_end * np.ones(num_diffusion_timesteps, dtype=np.float64)\n    warmup_time = int(num_diffusion_timesteps * warmup_frac)\n    betas[:warmup_time] = np.linspace(beta_start, beta_end, warmup_time, dtype=np.float64)\n    return betas\n\n\ndef get_beta_schedule(beta_schedule, *, beta_start, beta_end, num_diffusion_timesteps):\n    \"\"\"\n    This is the deprecated API for creating beta schedules.\n    See get_named_beta_schedule() for the new library of schedules.\n    \"\"\"\n    if beta_schedule == \"quad\":\n        betas = (\n            np.linspace(\n                beta_start ** 0.5,\n                beta_end ** 0.5,\n                num_diffusion_timesteps,\n                dtype=np.float64,\n            )\n            ** 2\n        )\n    elif beta_schedule == \"linear\":\n        betas = np.linspace(beta_start, beta_end, num_diffusion_timesteps, dtype=np.float64)\n    elif beta_schedule == \"warmup10\":\n        betas = _warmup_beta(beta_start, beta_end, num_diffusion_timesteps, 0.1)\n    elif beta_schedule == \"warmup50\":\n        betas = _warmup_beta(beta_start, beta_end, num_diffusion_timesteps, 0.5)\n    elif beta_schedule == \"const\":\n        betas = beta_end * np.ones(num_diffusion_timesteps, dtype=np.float64)\n    elif beta_schedule == \"jsd\":  # 1/T, 1/(T-1), 1/(T-2), ..., 1\n        betas = 1.0 / np.linspace(\n            num_diffusion_timesteps, 1, num_diffusion_timesteps, dtype=np.float64\n        )\n    else:\n        raise NotImplementedError(beta_schedule)\n    assert betas.shape == (num_diffusion_timesteps,)\n    return betas\n\n\ndef get_named_beta_schedule(schedule_name, num_diffusion_timesteps):\n    \"\"\"\n    Get a pre-defined beta schedule for the given name.\n    The beta schedule library consists of beta schedules which remain similar\n    in the limit of num_diffusion_timesteps.\n    Beta schedules may be added, but should not be removed or changed once\n    they are committed to maintain backwards compatibility.\n    \"\"\"\n    if schedule_name == \"linear\":\n        # Linear schedule from Ho et al, extended to work for any number of\n        # diffusion steps.\n        scale = 1000 / num_diffusion_timesteps\n        return get_beta_schedule(\n            \"linear\",\n            beta_start=scale * 0.0001,\n            beta_end=scale * 0.02,\n            num_diffusion_timesteps=num_diffusion_timesteps,\n        )\n    elif schedule_name == \"squaredcos_cap_v2\":\n        return betas_for_alpha_bar(\n            num_diffusion_timesteps,\n            lambda t: math.cos((t + 0.008) / 1.008 * math.pi / 2) ** 2,\n        )\n    else:\n        raise NotImplementedError(f\"unknown beta schedule: {schedule_name}\")\n\n\ndef betas_for_alpha_bar(num_diffusion_timesteps, alpha_bar, max_beta=0.999):\n    \"\"\"\n    Create a beta schedule that discretizes the given alpha_t_bar function,\n    which defines the cumulative product of (1-beta) over time from t = [0,1].\n    :param num_diffusion_timesteps: the number of betas to produce.\n    :param alpha_bar: a lambda that takes an argument t from 0 to 1 and\n                      produces the cumulative product of (1-beta) up to that\n                      part of the diffusion process.\n    :param max_beta: the maximum beta to use; use values lower than 1 to\n                     prevent singularities.\n    \"\"\"\n    betas = []\n    for i in range(num_diffusion_timesteps):\n        t1 = i / num_diffusion_timesteps\n        t2 = (i + 1) / num_diffusion_timesteps\n        betas.append(min(1 - alpha_bar(t2) / alpha_bar(t1), max_beta))\n    return np.array(betas)\n\n\nclass GaussianDiffusion:\n    \"\"\"\n    Utilities for training and sampling diffusion models.\n    Original ported from this codebase:\n    https://github.com/hojonathanho/diffusion/blob/1e0dceb3b3495bbe19116a5e1b3596cd0706c543/diffusion_tf/diffusion_utils_2.py#L42\n    :param betas: a 1-D numpy array of betas for each diffusion timestep,\n                  starting at T and going to 1.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(\n        self,\n        *,\n        betas,\n        model_mean_type,\n        model_var_type,\n        loss_type\n    ):\n\n        self.model_mean_type = model_mean_type\n        self.model_var_type = model_var_type\n        self.loss_type = loss_type\n\n        # Use float64 for accuracy.\n        betas = np.array(betas, dtype=np.float64)\n        self.betas = betas\n        assert len(betas.shape) == 1, \"betas must be 1-D\"\n        assert (betas > 0).all() and (betas <= 1).all()\n\n        self.num_timesteps = int(betas.shape[0])\n\n        alphas = 1.0 - betas\n        self.alphas_cumprod = np.cumprod(alphas, axis=0)\n        self.alphas_cumprod_prev = np.append(1.0, self.alphas_cumprod[:-1])\n        self.alphas_cumprod_next = np.append(self.alphas_cumprod[1:], 0.0)\n        assert self.alphas_cumprod_prev.shape == (self.num_timesteps,)\n\n        # calculations for diffusion q(x_t | x_{t-1}) and others\n        self.sqrt_alphas_cumprod = np.sqrt(self.alphas_cumprod)\n        self.sqrt_one_minus_alphas_cumprod = np.sqrt(1.0 - self.alphas_cumprod)\n        self.log_one_minus_alphas_cumprod = np.log(1.0 - self.alphas_cumprod)\n        self.sqrt_recip_alphas_cumprod = np.sqrt(1.0 / self.alphas_cumprod)\n        self.sqrt_recipm1_alphas_cumprod = np.sqrt(1.0 / self.alphas_cumprod - 1)\n\n        # calculations for posterior q(x_{t-1} | x_t, x_0)\n        self.posterior_variance = (\n            betas * (1.0 - self.alphas_cumprod_prev) / (1.0 - self.alphas_cumprod)\n        )\n        # below: log calculation clipped because the posterior variance is 0 at the beginning of the diffusion chain\n        self.posterior_log_variance_clipped = np.log(\n            np.append(self.posterior_variance[1], self.posterior_variance[1:])\n        ) if len(self.posterior_variance) > 1 else np.array([])\n\n        self.posterior_mean_coef1 = (\n            betas * np.sqrt(self.alphas_cumprod_prev) / (1.0 - self.alphas_cumprod)\n        )\n        self.posterior_mean_coef2 = (\n            (1.0 - self.alphas_cumprod_prev) * np.sqrt(alphas) / (1.0 - self.alphas_cumprod)\n        )\n\n    def q_mean_variance(self, x_start, t):\n        \"\"\"\n        Get the distribution q(x_t | x_0).\n        :param x_start: the [N x C x ...] tensor of noiseless inputs.\n        :param t: the number of diffusion steps (minus 1). Here, 0 means one step.\n        :return: A tuple (mean, variance, log_variance), all of x_start's shape.\n        \"\"\"\n        mean = _extract_into_tensor(self.sqrt_alphas_cumprod, t, x_start.shape) * x_start\n        variance = _extract_into_tensor(1.0 - self.alphas_cumprod, t, x_start.shape)\n        log_variance = _extract_into_tensor(self.log_one_minus_alphas_cumprod, t, x_start.shape)\n        return mean, variance, log_variance\n\n    def q_sample(self, x_start, t, noise=None):\n        \"\"\"\n        Diffuse the data for a given number of diffusion steps.\n        In other words, sample from q(x_t | x_0).\n        :param x_start: the initial data batch.\n        :param t: the number of diffusion steps (minus 1). Here, 0 means one step.\n        :param noise: if specified, the split-out normal noise.\n        :return: A noisy version of x_start.\n        \"\"\"\n        if noise is None:\n            noise = th.randn_like(x_start)\n        assert noise.shape == x_start.shape\n        return (\n            _extract_into_tensor(self.sqrt_alphas_cumprod, t, x_start.shape) * x_start\n            + _extract_into_tensor(self.sqrt_one_minus_alphas_cumprod, t, x_start.shape) * noise\n        )\n\n    def q_posterior_mean_variance(self, x_start, x_t, t):\n        \"\"\"\n        Compute the mean and variance of the diffusion posterior:\n            q(x_{t-1} | x_t, x_0)\n        \"\"\"\n        assert x_start.shape == x_t.shape\n        posterior_mean = (\n            _extract_into_tensor(self.posterior_mean_coef1, t, x_t.shape) * x_start\n            + _extract_into_tensor(self.posterior_mean_coef2, t, x_t.shape) * x_t\n        )\n        posterior_variance = _extract_into_tensor(self.posterior_variance, t, x_t.shape)\n        posterior_log_variance_clipped = _extract_into_tensor(\n            self.posterior_log_variance_clipped, t, x_t.shape\n        )\n        assert (\n            posterior_mean.shape[0]\n            == posterior_variance.shape[0]\n            == posterior_log_variance_clipped.shape[0]\n            == x_start.shape[0]\n        )\n        return posterior_mean, posterior_variance, posterior_log_variance_clipped\n\n    def p_mean_variance(self, model, x, t, clip_denoised=True, denoised_fn=None, model_kwargs=None):\n        \"\"\"\n        Apply the model to get p(x_{t-1} | x_t), as well as a prediction of\n        the initial x, x_0.\n        :param model: the model, which takes a signal and a batch of timesteps\n                      as input.\n        :param x: the [N x C x ...] tensor at time t.\n        :param t: a 1-D Tensor of timesteps.\n        :param clip_denoised: if True, clip the denoised signal into [-1, 1].\n        :param denoised_fn: if not None, a function which applies to the\n            x_start prediction before it is used to sample. Applies before\n            clip_denoised.\n        :param model_kwargs: if not None, a dict of extra keyword arguments to\n            pass to the model. This can be used for conditioning.\n        :return: a dict with the following keys:\n                 - 'mean': the model mean output.\n                 - 'variance': the model variance output.\n                 - 'log_variance': the log of 'variance'.\n                 - 'pred_xstart': the prediction for x_0.\n        \"\"\"\n        if model_kwargs is None:\n            model_kwargs = {}\n\n        B, C = x.shape[:2]\n        assert t.shape == (B,)\n        model_output = model(x, t, **model_kwargs)\n        if isinstance(model_output, tuple):\n            model_output, extra = model_output\n        else:\n            extra = None\n\n        if self.model_var_type in [ModelVarType.LEARNED, ModelVarType.LEARNED_RANGE]:\n            assert model_output.shape == (B, C * 2, *x.shape[2:])\n            model_output, model_var_values = th.split(model_output, C, dim=1)\n            min_log = _extract_into_tensor(self.posterior_log_variance_clipped, t, x.shape)\n            max_log = _extract_into_tensor(np.log(self.betas), t, x.shape)\n            # The model_var_values is [-1, 1] for [min_var, max_var].\n            frac = (model_var_values + 1) / 2\n            model_log_variance = frac * max_log + (1 - frac) * min_log\n            model_variance = th.exp(model_log_variance)\n        else:\n            model_variance, model_log_variance = {\n                # for fixedlarge, we set the initial (log-)variance like so\n                # to get a better decoder log likelihood.\n                ModelVarType.FIXED_LARGE: (\n                    np.append(self.posterior_variance[1], self.betas[1:]),\n                    np.log(np.append(self.posterior_variance[1], self.betas[1:])),\n                ),\n                ModelVarType.FIXED_SMALL: (\n                    self.posterior_variance,\n                    self.posterior_log_variance_clipped,\n                ),\n            }[self.model_var_type]\n            model_variance = _extract_into_tensor(model_variance, t, x.shape)\n            model_log_variance = _extract_into_tensor(model_log_variance, t, x.shape)\n\n        def process_xstart(x):\n            if denoised_fn is not None:\n                x = denoised_fn(x)\n            if clip_denoised:\n                return x.clamp(-1, 1)\n            return x\n\n        if self.model_mean_type == ModelMeanType.START_X:\n            pred_xstart = process_xstart(model_output)\n        else:\n            pred_xstart = process_xstart(\n                self._predict_xstart_from_eps(x_t=x, t=t, eps=model_output)\n            )\n        model_mean, _, _ = self.q_posterior_mean_variance(x_start=pred_xstart, x_t=x, t=t)\n\n        assert model_mean.shape == model_log_variance.shape == pred_xstart.shape == x.shape\n        return {\n            \"mean\": model_mean,\n            \"variance\": model_variance,\n            \"log_variance\": model_log_variance,\n            \"pred_xstart\": pred_xstart,\n            \"extra\": extra,\n        }\n\n    def _predict_xstart_from_eps(self, x_t, t, eps):\n        assert x_t.shape == eps.shape\n        return (\n            _extract_into_tensor(self.sqrt_recip_alphas_cumprod, t, x_t.shape) * x_t\n            - _extract_into_tensor(self.sqrt_recipm1_alphas_cumprod, t, x_t.shape) * eps\n        )\n\n    def _predict_eps_from_xstart(self, x_t, t, pred_xstart):\n        return (\n            _extract_into_tensor(self.sqrt_recip_alphas_cumprod, t, x_t.shape) * x_t - pred_xstart\n        ) / _extract_into_tensor(self.sqrt_recipm1_alphas_cumprod, t, x_t.shape)\n\n    def condition_mean(self, cond_fn, p_mean_var, x, t, model_kwargs=None):\n        \"\"\"\n        Compute the mean for the previous step, given a function cond_fn that\n        computes the gradient of a conditional log probability with respect to\n        x. In particular, cond_fn computes grad(log(p(y|x))), and we want to\n        condition on y.\n        This uses the conditioning strategy from Sohl-Dickstein et al. (2015).\n        \"\"\"\n        gradient = cond_fn(x, t, **model_kwargs)\n        new_mean = p_mean_var[\"mean\"].float() + p_mean_var[\"variance\"] * gradient.float()\n        return new_mean\n\n    def condition_score(self, cond_fn, p_mean_var, x, t, model_kwargs=None):\n        \"\"\"\n        Compute what the p_mean_variance output would have been, should the\n        model's score function be conditioned by cond_fn.\n        See condition_mean() for details on cond_fn.\n        Unlike condition_mean(), this instead uses the conditioning strategy\n        from Song et al (2020).\n        \"\"\"\n        alpha_bar = _extract_into_tensor(self.alphas_cumprod, t, x.shape)\n\n        eps = self._predict_eps_from_xstart(x, t, p_mean_var[\"pred_xstart\"])\n        eps = eps - (1 - alpha_bar).sqrt() * cond_fn(x, t, **model_kwargs)\n\n        out = p_mean_var.copy()\n        out[\"pred_xstart\"] = self._predict_xstart_from_eps(x, t, eps)\n        out[\"mean\"], _, _ = self.q_posterior_mean_variance(x_start=out[\"pred_xstart\"], x_t=x, t=t)\n        return out\n\n    def p_sample(\n        self,\n        model,\n        x,\n        t,\n        clip_denoised=True,\n        denoised_fn=None,\n        cond_fn=None,\n        model_kwargs=None,\n    ):\n        \"\"\"\n        Sample x_{t-1} from the model at the given timestep.\n        :param model: the model to sample from.\n        :param x: the current tensor at x_{t-1}.\n        :param t: the value of t, starting at 0 for the first diffusion step.\n        :param clip_denoised: if True, clip the x_start prediction to [-1, 1].\n        :param denoised_fn: if not None, a function which applies to the\n            x_start prediction before it is used to sample.\n        :param cond_fn: if not None, this is a gradient function that acts\n                        similarly to the model.\n        :param model_kwargs: if not None, a dict of extra keyword arguments to\n            pass to the model. This can be used for conditioning.\n        :return: a dict containing the following keys:\n                 - 'sample': a random sample from the model.\n                 - 'pred_xstart': a prediction of x_0.\n        \"\"\"\n        out = self.p_mean_variance(\n            model,\n            x,\n            t,\n            clip_denoised=clip_denoised,\n            denoised_fn=denoised_fn,\n            model_kwargs=model_kwargs,\n        )\n        noise = th.randn_like(x)\n        nonzero_mask = (\n            (t != 0).float().view(-1, *([1] * (len(x.shape) - 1)))\n        )  # no noise when t == 0\n        if cond_fn is not None:\n            out[\"mean\"] = self.condition_mean(cond_fn, out, x, t, model_kwargs=model_kwargs)\n        sample = out[\"mean\"] + nonzero_mask * th.exp(0.5 * out[\"log_variance\"]) * noise\n        return {\"sample\": sample, \"pred_xstart\": out[\"pred_xstart\"]}\n\n    def p_sample_loop(\n        self,\n        model,\n        shape,\n        noise=None,\n        clip_denoised=True,\n        denoised_fn=None,\n        cond_fn=None,\n        model_kwargs=None,\n        device=None,\n        progress=False,\n    ):\n        \"\"\"\n        Generate samples from the model.\n        :param model: the model module.\n        :param shape: the shape of the samples, (N, C, H, W).\n        :param noise: if specified, the noise from the encoder to sample.\n                      Should be of the same shape as `shape`.\n        :param clip_denoised: if True, clip x_start predictions to [-1, 1].\n        :param denoised_fn: if not None, a function which applies to the\n            x_start prediction before it is used to sample.\n        :param cond_fn: if not None, this is a gradient function that acts\n                        similarly to the model.\n        :param model_kwargs: if not None, a dict of extra keyword arguments to\n            pass to the model. This can be used for conditioning.\n        :param device: if specified, the device to create the samples on.\n                       If not specified, use a model parameter's device.\n        :param progress: if True, show a tqdm progress bar.\n        :return: a non-differentiable batch of samples.\n        \"\"\"\n        final = None\n        for sample in self.p_sample_loop_progressive(\n            model,\n            shape,\n            noise=noise,\n            clip_denoised=clip_denoised,\n            denoised_fn=denoised_fn,\n            cond_fn=cond_fn,\n            model_kwargs=model_kwargs,\n            device=device,\n            progress=progress,\n        ):\n            final = sample\n        return final[\"sample\"]\n\n    def p_sample_loop_progressive(\n        self,\n        model,\n        shape,\n        noise=None,\n        clip_denoised=True,\n        denoised_fn=None,\n        cond_fn=None,\n        model_kwargs=None,\n        device=None,\n        progress=False,\n    ):\n        \"\"\"\n        Generate samples from the model and yield intermediate samples from\n        each timestep of diffusion.\n        Arguments are the same as p_sample_loop().\n        Returns a generator over dicts, where each dict is the return value of\n        p_sample().\n        \"\"\"\n        if device is None:\n            device = next(model.parameters()).device\n        assert isinstance(shape, (tuple, list))\n        if noise is not None:\n            img = noise\n        else:\n            img = th.randn(*shape, device=device)\n        indices = list(range(self.num_timesteps))[::-1]\n\n        if progress:\n            # Lazy import so that we don't depend on tqdm.\n            from tqdm.auto import tqdm\n\n            indices = tqdm(indices)\n\n        for i in indices:\n            t = th.tensor([i] * shape[0], device=device)\n            with th.no_grad():\n                out = self.p_sample(\n                    model,\n                    img,\n                    t,\n                    clip_denoised=clip_denoised,\n                    denoised_fn=denoised_fn,\n                    cond_fn=cond_fn,\n                    model_kwargs=model_kwargs,\n                )\n                yield out\n                img = out[\"sample\"]\n\n    def ddim_sample(\n        self,\n        model,\n        x,\n        t,\n        clip_denoised=True,\n        denoised_fn=None,\n        cond_fn=None,\n        model_kwargs=None,\n        eta=0.0,\n    ):\n        \"\"\"\n        Sample x_{t-1} from the model using DDIM.\n        Same usage as p_sample().\n        \"\"\"\n        out = self.p_mean_variance(\n            model,\n            x,\n            t,\n            clip_denoised=clip_denoised,\n            denoised_fn=denoised_fn,\n            model_kwargs=model_kwargs,\n        )\n        if cond_fn is not None:\n            out = self.condition_score(cond_fn, out, x, t, model_kwargs=model_kwargs)\n\n        # Usually our model outputs epsilon, but we re-derive it\n        # in case we used x_start or x_prev prediction.\n        eps = self._predict_eps_from_xstart(x, t, out[\"pred_xstart\"])\n\n        alpha_bar = _extract_into_tensor(self.alphas_cumprod, t, x.shape)\n        alpha_bar_prev = _extract_into_tensor(self.alphas_cumprod_prev, t, x.shape)\n        sigma = (\n            eta\n            * th.sqrt((1 - alpha_bar_prev) / (1 - alpha_bar))\n            * th.sqrt(1 - alpha_bar / alpha_bar_prev)\n        )\n        # Equation 12.\n        noise = th.randn_like(x)\n        mean_pred = (\n            out[\"pred_xstart\"] * th.sqrt(alpha_bar_prev)\n            + th.sqrt(1 - alpha_bar_prev - sigma ** 2) * eps\n        )\n        nonzero_mask = (\n            (t != 0).float().view(-1, *([1] * (len(x.shape) - 1)))\n        )  # no noise when t == 0\n        sample = mean_pred + nonzero_mask * sigma * noise\n        return {\"sample\": sample, \"pred_xstart\": out[\"pred_xstart\"]}\n\n    def ddim_reverse_sample(\n        self,\n        model,\n        x,\n        t,\n        clip_denoised=True,\n        denoised_fn=None,\n        cond_fn=None,\n        model_kwargs=None,\n        eta=0.0,\n    ):\n        \"\"\"\n        Sample x_{t+1} from the model using DDIM reverse ODE.\n        \"\"\"\n        assert eta == 0.0, \"Reverse ODE only for deterministic path\"\n        out = self.p_mean_variance(\n            model,\n            x,\n            t,\n            clip_denoised=clip_denoised,\n            denoised_fn=denoised_fn,\n            model_kwargs=model_kwargs,\n        )\n        if cond_fn is not None:\n            out = self.condition_score(cond_fn, out, x, t, model_kwargs=model_kwargs)\n        # Usually our model outputs epsilon, but we re-derive it\n        # in case we used x_start or x_prev prediction.\n        eps = (\n            _extract_into_tensor(self.sqrt_recip_alphas_cumprod, t, x.shape) * x\n            - out[\"pred_xstart\"]\n        ) / _extract_into_tensor(self.sqrt_recipm1_alphas_cumprod, t, x.shape)\n        alpha_bar_next = _extract_into_tensor(self.alphas_cumprod_next, t, x.shape)\n\n        # Equation 12. reversed\n        mean_pred = out[\"pred_xstart\"] * th.sqrt(alpha_bar_next) + th.sqrt(1 - alpha_bar_next) * eps\n\n        return {\"sample\": mean_pred, \"pred_xstart\": out[\"pred_xstart\"]}\n\n    def ddim_sample_loop(\n        self,\n        model,\n        shape,\n        noise=None,\n        clip_denoised=True,\n        denoised_fn=None,\n        cond_fn=None,\n        model_kwargs=None,\n        device=None,\n        progress=False,\n        eta=0.0,\n    ):\n        \"\"\"\n        Generate samples from the model using DDIM.\n        Same usage as p_sample_loop().\n        \"\"\"\n        final = None\n        for sample in self.ddim_sample_loop_progressive(\n            model,\n            shape,\n            noise=noise,\n            clip_denoised=clip_denoised,\n            denoised_fn=denoised_fn,\n            cond_fn=cond_fn,\n            model_kwargs=model_kwargs,\n            device=device,\n            progress=progress,\n            eta=eta,\n        ):\n            final = sample\n        return final[\"sample\"]\n\n    def ddim_sample_loop_progressive(\n        self,\n        model,\n        shape,\n        noise=None,\n        clip_denoised=True,\n        denoised_fn=None,\n        cond_fn=None,\n        model_kwargs=None,\n        device=None,\n        progress=False,\n        eta=0.0,\n    ):\n        \"\"\"\n        Use DDIM to sample from the model and yield intermediate samples from\n        each timestep of DDIM.\n        Same usage as p_sample_loop_progressive().\n        \"\"\"\n        if device is None:\n            device = next(model.parameters()).device\n        assert isinstance(shape, (tuple, list))\n        if noise is not None:\n            img = noise\n        else:\n            img = th.randn(*shape, device=device)\n        indices = list(range(self.num_timesteps))[::-1]\n\n        if progress:\n            # Lazy import so that we don't depend on tqdm.\n            from tqdm.auto import tqdm\n\n            indices = tqdm(indices)\n\n        for i in indices:\n            t = th.tensor([i] * shape[0], device=device)\n            with th.no_grad():\n                out = self.ddim_sample(\n                    model,\n                    img,\n                    t,\n                    clip_denoised=clip_denoised,\n                    denoised_fn=denoised_fn,\n                    cond_fn=cond_fn,\n                    model_kwargs=model_kwargs,\n                    eta=eta,\n                )\n                yield out\n                img = out[\"sample\"]\n\n    def _vb_terms_bpd(\n            self, model, x_start, x_t, t, clip_denoised=True, model_kwargs=None\n    ):\n        \"\"\"\n        Get a term for the variational lower-bound.\n        The resulting units are bits (rather than nats, as one might expect).\n        This allows for comparison to other papers.\n        :return: a dict with the following keys:\n                 - 'output': a shape [N] tensor of NLLs or KLs.\n                 - 'pred_xstart': the x_0 predictions.\n        \"\"\"\n        true_mean, _, true_log_variance_clipped = self.q_posterior_mean_variance(\n            x_start=x_start, x_t=x_t, t=t\n        )\n        out = self.p_mean_variance(\n            model, x_t, t, clip_denoised=clip_denoised, model_kwargs=model_kwargs\n        )\n        kl = normal_kl(\n            true_mean, true_log_variance_clipped, out[\"mean\"], out[\"log_variance\"]\n        )\n        kl = mean_flat(kl) / np.log(2.0)\n\n        decoder_nll = -continuous_gaussian_log_likelihood(\n            x_start, means=out[\"mean\"], log_scales=0.5 * out[\"log_variance\"]\n        )\n        assert decoder_nll.shape == x_start.shape\n        decoder_nll = mean_flat(decoder_nll) / np.log(2.0)\n\n        # At the first timestep return the decoder NLL,\n        # otherwise return KL(q(x_{t-1}|x_t,x_0) || p(x_{t-1}|x_t))\n        output = th.where((t == 0), decoder_nll, kl)\n        return {\"output\": output, \"pred_xstart\": out[\"pred_xstart\"]}\n\n    def training_losses(self, model, x_start, x_ori,  t, noise=None):\n        \"\"\"\n        Compute training losses for a single timestep.\n        :param model: the model to evaluate loss on.\n        :param x_start: the [N x C x ...] tensor of inputs.\n        :param t: a batch of timestep indices.\n        :param model_kwargs: if not None, a dict of extra keyword arguments to\n            pass to the model. This can be used for conditioning.\n        :param noise: if specified, the specific Gaussian noise to try to remove.\n        :return: a dict with the key \"loss\" containing a tensor of shape [N].\n                 Some mean or variance settings may also have other keys.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        if noise is None:\n            noise = th.randn_like(x_start)\n        x_t = self.q_sample(x_start, t, noise=noise)\n\n        terms = {}\n\n        if self.loss_type == LossType.KL or self.loss_type == LossType.RESCALED_KL:\n            terms[\"loss\"] = self._vb_terms_bpd(\n                model=model,\n                x_start=x_start,\n                x_t=x_t,\n                t=t,\n                clip_denoised=False,\n            )[\"output\"]\n            if self.loss_type == LossType.RESCALED_KL:\n                terms[\"loss\"] *= self.num_timesteps\n        elif self.loss_type == LossType.MSE or self.loss_type == LossType.RESCALED_MSE:\n            model_output = model(x_t, x_ori, t)\n            if self.model_var_type in [\n                ModelVarType.LEARNED,\n                ModelVarType.LEARNED_RANGE,\n            ]:\n                B, C = x_t.shape[:2]\n                assert model_output.shape == (B, C * 2, *x_t.shape[2:])\n                model_output, model_var_values = th.split(model_output, C, dim=1)\n                # Learn the variance using the variational bound, but don't let\n                # it affect our mean prediction.\n                frozen_out = th.cat([model_output.detach(), model_var_values], dim=1)\n                terms[\"vb\"] = self._vb_terms_bpd(\n                    model=lambda *args, r=frozen_out: r,\n                    x_start=x_start,\n                    x_t=x_t,\n                    t=t,\n                    clip_denoised=False,\n                )[\"output\"]\n                if self.loss_type == LossType.RESCALED_MSE:\n                    # Divide by 1000 for equivalence with initial implementation.\n                    # Without a factor of 1/1000, the VB term hurts the MSE term.\n                    terms[\"vb\"] *= self.num_timesteps / 1000.0\n\n            target = {\n                ModelMeanType.PREVIOUS_X: self.q_posterior_mean_variance(\n                    x_start=x_start, x_t=x_t, t=t\n                )[0],\n                ModelMeanType.START_X: x_start,\n                ModelMeanType.EPSILON: noise,\n            }[self.model_mean_type]\n            assert model_output[:, 0, :].shape == target.shape == x_start.shape\n            terms[\"mse\"] = mean_flat((target - model_output[:, 0, :]) ** 2)\n            if \"vb\" in terms:\n                terms[\"loss\"] = terms[\"mse\"] + terms[\"vb\"]\n            else:\n                terms[\"loss\"] = terms[\"mse\"]\n        else:\n            raise NotImplementedError(self.loss_type)\n\n        return terms[\"loss\"], model_output\n\n    def _prior_bpd(self, x_start):\n        \"\"\"\n        Get the prior KL term for the variational lower-bound, measured in\n        bits-per-dim.\n        This term can't be optimized, as it only depends on the encoder.\n        :param x_start: the [N x C x ...] tensor of inputs.\n        :return: a batch of [N] KL values (in bits), one per batch element.\n        \"\"\"\n        batch_size = x_start.shape[0]\n        t = th.tensor([self.num_timesteps - 1] * batch_size, device=x_start.device)\n        qt_mean, _, qt_log_variance = self.q_mean_variance(x_start, t)\n        kl_prior = normal_kl(\n            mean1=qt_mean, logvar1=qt_log_variance, mean2=0.0, logvar2=0.0\n        )\n        return mean_flat(kl_prior) / np.log(2.0)\n\n    def calc_bpd_loop(self, model, x_start, clip_denoised=True, model_kwargs=None):\n        \"\"\"\n        Compute the entire variational lower-bound, measured in bits-per-dim,\n        as well as other related quantities.\n        :param model: the model to evaluate loss on.\n        :param x_start: the [N x C x ...] tensor of inputs.\n        :param clip_denoised: if True, clip denoised samples.\n        :param model_kwargs: if not None, a dict of extra keyword arguments to\n            pass to the model. This can be used for conditioning.\n        :return: a dict containing the following keys:\n                 - total_bpd: the total variational lower-bound, per batch element.\n                 - prior_bpd: the prior term in the lower-bound.\n                 - vb: an [N x T] tensor of terms in the lower-bound.\n                 - xstart_mse: an [N x T] tensor of x_0 MSEs for each timestep.\n                 - mse: an [N x T] tensor of epsilon MSEs for each timestep.\n        \"\"\"\n        device = x_start.device\n        batch_size = x_start.shape[0]\n\n        vb = []\n        xstart_mse = []\n        mse = []\n        for t in list(range(self.num_timesteps))[::-1]:\n            t_batch = th.tensor([t] * batch_size, device=device)\n            noise = th.randn_like(x_start)\n            x_t = self.q_sample(x_start=x_start, t=t_batch, noise=noise)\n            # Calculate VLB term at the current timestep\n            with th.no_grad():\n                out = self._vb_terms_bpd(\n                    model,\n                    x_start=x_start,\n                    x_t=x_t,\n                    t=t_batch,\n                    clip_denoised=clip_denoised,\n                    model_kwargs=model_kwargs,\n                )\n            vb.append(out[\"output\"])\n            xstart_mse.append(mean_flat((out[\"pred_xstart\"] - x_start) ** 2))\n            eps = self._predict_eps_from_xstart(x_t, t_batch, out[\"pred_xstart\"])\n            mse.append(mean_flat((eps - noise) ** 2))\n\n        vb = th.stack(vb, dim=1)\n        xstart_mse = th.stack(xstart_mse, dim=1)\n        mse = th.stack(mse, dim=1)\n\n        prior_bpd = self._prior_bpd(x_start)\n        total_bpd = vb.sum(dim=1) + prior_bpd\n        return {\n            \"total_bpd\": total_bpd,\n            \"prior_bpd\": prior_bpd,\n            \"vb\": vb,\n            \"xstart_mse\": xstart_mse,\n            \"mse\": mse,\n        }\n\n\ndef _extract_into_tensor(arr, timesteps, broadcast_shape):\n    \"\"\"\n    Extract values from a 1-D numpy array for a batch of indices.\n    :param arr: the 1-D numpy array.\n    :param timesteps: a tensor of indices into the array to extract.\n    :param broadcast_shape: a larger shape of K dimensions with the batch\n                            dimension equal to the length of timesteps.\n    :return: a tensor of shape [batch_size, 1, ...] where the shape has K dims.\n    \"\"\"\n    res = th.from_numpy(arr).to(device=timesteps.device)[timesteps].float()\n    while len(res.shape) < len(broadcast_shape):\n        res = res[..., None]\n    return res + th.zeros(broadcast_shape, device=timesteps.device)\n\nFile Path: utils/sampling.py\nContent:\n\nimport torch\nimport torch.nn.functional as F\n\n\n\ndef generalized_steps(model, x, time, numstep=10):\n    with torch.no_grad():\n        num_steps = numstep\n        skip = time // num_steps\n        epi = torch.randn_like(x[:, 0, :])\n        seq = range(0, time, skip)\n        n = epi.size(0)\n        seq_next = [-1] + list(seq[:-1])\n        xs = [epi]\n\n        for i, j in zip(reversed(seq), reversed(seq_next)):\n            t = (torch.ones(n) * i).to(x.device)\n            xt = xs[-1].to('cuda')\n            x0_t = model(xt, x, t)\n            xs.append(x0_t[:, 0, :])\n        final_prototype = xs[-1]\n\n\n    return final_prototype\n",
    "Experiment Result": "Method: ProtoDiff\n\nDatasets:\n  - Training: mini_imagenet (from config, or any of ['ilsvrc_2012', 'omniglot', 'aircraft', 'cu_birds', 'dtd', 'quickdraw', 'fungi', 'vgg_flower'] if args.dataset == 'all')\n  -     "Title": "Learning a Universal Template for Few-shot Dataset Generalization",
    "Main Contributions": "The paper addresses the challenging problem of few-shot dataset generalization, where a model trained on diverse datasets must learn new classes from previously-unseen datasets with few examples. The main contribution is FLUTE (Few-shot Learning with a Universal TEmplate), a novel approach that constructs a universal template (shared convolutional weights) and learns dataset-specific FiLM parameters (conditional batch normalization). FLUTE is more parameter-efficient, scalable, and adaptable than previous methods, achieving state-of-the-art performance on the Meta-Dataset benchmark by a significant margin (>5% on average strong generalization) with approximately 8 times fewer parameters.",
    "Methodology": "FLUTE employs a feature extractor (ResNet-18) where convolutional layer parameters form the universal template (Φ) and dataset-specific FiLM parameters (Ψ) are integrated via conditional batch normalization. During training, Φ and M sets of Ψ (one per training dataset) are jointly optimized using a multi-task cross-entropy loss with cosine classification readout heads. A separate Blender network, comprising a permutation-invariant set encoder and a linear readout, is trained as a dataset classifier to estimate compatibility between new tasks and training datasets. For test tasks from unseen datasets, the Blender network generates an initialization (Ψinit d*) for new FiLM parameters by taking a convex combination of the trained Ψ parameters. These Ψd* parameters are then fine-tuned via a few steps of gradient descent on the task's support set, minimizing a Nearest-Centroid Classifier (NCC) loss. Only Ψd* is updated during fine-tuning, while Φ remains frozen. Finally, the NCC classifies query examples using the learned Ψd*.",
    "Experimental Setup": "Experiments were conducted on the Meta-Dataset benchmark, which includes 8 diverse training datasets (ImageNet, Omniglot, Aircraft, Birds, Flowers, Quickdraw, Fungi, Textures) and 2 test datasets (Traffic Signs, MSCOCO). Following prior work, 3 additional evaluation-only datasets (MNIST, CIFAR-10, CIFAR-100) were incorporated for a richer test set, evaluating both strong generalization (unseen datasets) and weak generalization (held-out classes from seen datasets). A ResNet-18 architecture was used for the feature extractor. Training involved stochastic gradient descent with momentum for the universal template and FiLM parameters (cosine decay with restarts, initial LR 0.01, weight decay 7e-4 for conv layers, 0.001 for FiLM). The dataset classifier was trained with Adam (cosine decay, initial LR 0.001, early-stopped based on validation accuracy). Fine-tuning of Ψd* used Adam with a fixed learning rate of 0.005 for 6 steps. Evaluation metric was average query set accuracy over 600 test tasks, with 95% confidence intervals and statistical hypothesis testing for ranking.",
    "Limitations": "One limitation is the challenge of model selection in few-shot dataset generalization, as the validation set may not be a good proxy for the test set. This leads to an underspecification problem where different solutions performing equally well on training/validation sets may behave differently on unseen test sets. The results can be sensitive to the chosen checkpoint of the dataset classifier and initialization of the FiLM parameters (Ψd*). Furthermore, the fixed hyperparameters for fine-tuning (learning rate and number of steps) may not be ideal for all test tasks, sometimes even leading to a decrease in support accuracy.",
    "Future Research Directions": "Future research could explore tailoring the learning rate or the number of fine-tuning steps (for Ψd*) to each individual task. The paper also implicitly suggests the importance of robust evaluation metrics and reporting variance across multiple runs to account for the inherent underspecification in few-shot dataset generalization.",
    "Experiment Code": "class FLUTEFiLMLearner(learner_base.EpisodicLearner):\n\n  def __init__(self, num_steps, lr, film_init, debug_log=False, **kwargs):\n    if film_init not in ['scratch', 'imagenet', 'blender', 'blender_hard']:\n      raise ValueError('Unknown FiLM parameter init scheme.')\n    tf.logging.info(\n        'Initializing a FiLM Learner with {} steps and lr {}'.format(\n super(FLUTEFiLMLearner, self).__init__(**kwargs)\n    delattr(self, 'logit_dim')\n\n  def forward_pass(self, data):\n\n    if self.film_init == 'scratch':\n      self.film_selector = None\n    elif self.film_init == 'imagenet':\n      self.film_selector = 0\n    elif self.film_init in ['blender', 'blender_hard']:\n      dataset_logits = functional_backbones.dataset_classifier(\n          data.support_images)\n      if self.film_init == 'blender_hard':\n        self.film_selector = tf.one_hot(\n            tf.math.argmax(dataset_logits, axis=-1),\n            depth=tf.shape(dataset_logits)[1])\n      else:\n        self.film_selector = tf.nn.softmax(dataset_logits, axis=-1)\n\n    if self.num_steps:\n      loss = self._compute_losses(data, compute_on_query=False)['loss']\n\n      self.opt_vars = []\n      for var in tf.trainable_variables():\n        if '_for_film_learner' in var.name:\n          self.opt_vars.append(var)\n      tf.logging.info('FiLMLearner will optimize vars: {}'.format(\n          self.opt_vars))\n\n    for i in range(self.num_steps):\n      if i == 0:\n        vars_reset = tf.variables_initializer(var_list=self.opt_vars)\n        with tf.variable_scope('adam_opt', reuse=tf.AUTO_REUSE):\n          unused_op = self.opt.minimize(loss, var_list=self.opt_vars)\n        adam_reset = tf.variables_initializer(self.opt.variables())\n\n        with tf.control_dependencies([vars_reset, adam_reset, loss] +\n                                     self.opt_vars):\n          print_op = tf.no_op()\n          if self.debug_log:\n            print_op = tf.print(\n                ['step: %d' % i, self.opt_vars[0][0], 'loss:', loss],\n                summarize=-1)\n\n          with tf.control_dependencies([print_op]):\n            results = self._get_train_op(data)\n            (train_op, loss, query_loss, acc,\n             query_acc) = (results['train_op'], results['loss'],\n                           results['query_loss'], results['acc'],\n                           results['query_acc'])\n\n      else:\n        with tf.control_dependencies([train_op, loss, acc] + self.opt_vars +\n                                     [query_loss, query_acc] *\n                                     int(self.debug_log)):\n\n          print_op = tf.no_op()\n          if self.debug_log:\n            print_list = [\n                '################',\n                'step: %d' % i,\n                self.opt_vars[0][0],\n                'support loss:',\n                loss,\n                'query loss:',\n                query_loss,\n                'support acc:',\n                acc,\n                'query acc:',\n                query_acc,\n            ]\n            print_op = tf.print(print_list)\n\n          with tf.control_dependencies([print_op]):\n            results = self._get_train_op(data)\n            (train_op, loss, query_loss, acc,\n             query_acc) = (results['train_op'], results['loss'],\n                           results['query_loss'], results['acc'],\n                           results['query_acc'])\n\n    dependency_list = [] if not self.num_steps else [train_op] + self.opt_vars\n    with tf.control_dependencies(dependency_list):\n      results = self._compute_losses(data, compute_on_query=True)\n      (loss, query_loss, query_logits, acc,\n       query_acc) = (results['loss'], results['query_loss'],\n                     results['query_logits'], results['acc'],\n                     results['query_acc'])\n\n      print_op = tf.no_op()\n      if self.debug_log:\n        print_op = tf.print([\n            'Done training',\n            'support loss:',\n            loss,\n            'query loss:',\n            query_loss,\n            'support acc:',\n            acc,\n            'query acc:',\n            query_acc,\n        ])\n      with tf.control_dependencies([print_op]):\n        query_logits = tf.identity(query_logits)\n\n    return query_logits\n\n  def _forward_pass(self, images, params=None, moments=None):\n    return self.embedding_fn(\n        images,\n        self.is_training,\n        params=params,\n        moments=moments,\n        film_selector=self.film_selector)\n\n  def _compute_prototype_loss(self,\n                              embeddings,\n                              labels,\n                              labels_one_hot,\n                              prototypes=None):\n    labels_dense = labels\n    if prototypes is None:\n      labels = tf.cast(labels_one_hot, tf.float32)\n      embeddings_ = tf.expand_dims(embeddings, 1)\n      labels = tf.expand_dims(labels, 2)\n      class_sums = tf.reduce_sum(labels * embeddings_, 0)\n      class_num_images = tf.reduce_sum(labels, 0)\n      prototypes = class_sums / class_num_images\n\n    embeddings = tf.nn.l2_normalize(embeddings, 1, epsilon=1e-3)\n    prototypes = tf.nn.l2_normalize(prototypes, 1, epsilon=1e-3)\n    logits = tf.matmul(embeddings, prototypes, transpose_b=True)\n\n    loss = self.compute_loss(labels_one_hot, logits)\n    acc = tf.reduce_mean(self.compute_accuracy(labels_dense, logits))\n    return loss, acc, prototypes, logits\n\n  def _compute_losses(self, data, compute_on_query=False):\n    support_dict     "Experiment Result": "FLUTE employs a feature extractor (ResNet-18) where convolutional layer parameters form the universal template (Φ) and dataset-specific FiLM parameters (Ψ) are integrated via conditional batch normalization. During training, Φ and M sets of Ψ (one per training dataset) are jointly optimized using a multi-task cross-entropy loss with cosine classification readout heads.\n\nThe `FLUTEFiLMLearner` is configured with `num_steps` for fine-tuning the FiLM parameters, a learning rate `lr`, and an initialization method `film_init`. The `film_init` can be 'scratch' (random initialization, ignoring reloaded parameters), 'imagenet' (using the first set of learned FiLM parameters, assumed to be ImageNet's), 'blender' (convex combination of trained Ψ parameters using a dataset classifier), or 'blender_hard' (using Ψ from the most likely dataset identified by a dataset classifier).\n\nThe Blender network, comprising a permutation-invariant set encoder (`deep_set_encoder`) and a linear readout, is trained as a dataset classifier to estimate compatibility between new tasks and training datasets.\n\nFor test tasks, the Blender network generates an initialization (Ψinit d*) for new FiLM parameters by taking a convex combination of the trained Ψ parameters (for 'blender' init) or selecting from the most compatible dataset (for 'blender_hard' init). These Ψd* parameters are then fine-tuned for `num_steps` using gradient descent (AdamOptimizer by default in `FLUTEFiLMLearner`) on the task's support set. The fine-tuning objective is a Nearest-Centroid Classifier (NCC) loss (implemented via cosine similarity based prototype classification in `_compute_prototype_loss`). Only Ψd* parameters (variables with '_for_film_learner' in their name) are updated during fine-tuning, while Φ (universal template) remains frozen.\n\nOverall training for FLUTE is managed by `Trainer_flute` with `num_updates` total updates. The meta-batch size (`meta_batch_size`) can be configured, and training can be distributed across `num_gpus`. Learning rate scheduling (`learn_rate_scheduler`, e.g., 'cosine_decay', 'cosine_decay_restarts', 'exponential_decay') and optimizer type (`optimizer_type`, e.g., 'adam', 'momentum') are configurable for the outer loop. There are options to `sample_half_from_imagenet` (sample twice as often from ImageNet) and restore specific checkpoints for the FLUTE model (`checkpoint_to_restore`) and the dataset classifier (`dataset_classifier_to_restore`). Evaluation is performed for `num_eval_episodes`."

    "Title": "FeCAM: Exploiting the Heterogeneity of Class Distributions in Exemplar-Free Continual Learning",
    "Main Contributions": "The paper addresses the challenges of exemplar-free Class-Incremental Learning (CIL), particularly catastrophic forgetting and the suboptimal performance of Euclidean distance-based classifiers for new classes in non-stationary data streams. The main contribution is the Feature Covariance-Aware Metric (FeCAM), a Bayes classifier that leverages anisotropic Mahalanobis distance to effectively model the heterogeneity of class feature distributions. FeCAM achieves state-of-the-art results across many-shot, few-shot, and domain-incremental CIL settings without requiring any training steps on new tasks, and generalizes to both many- and few-shot scenarios, unlike existing methods.",
    "Methodology": "FeCAM proposes using an optimal Bayesian classifier that models class feature distributions with a multivariate normal distribution N(µy, Σy), employing the anisotropic Mahalanobis distance for classification. Key techniques include: 1) **Covariance Matrix Approximation**: Computing a covariance matrix per class (Σy) or a common covariance matrix for all seen classes (Σ1:t). 2) **Correlation Normalization**: Normalizing covariance matrices to ensure comparable variances across different classes by making diagonal elements equal to 1. 3) **Covariance Shrinkage**: Using Σs = Σ + γ1V1I + γ2V2(1 − I) to obtain a full-rank, invertible covariance matrix, especially when samples are fewer than feature dimensions. 4) **Tukey's Ladder of Powers Transformation**: Applying a power transformation to feature vectors (˜ϕ(x) = ϕ(x)^λ or log(ϕ(x))) to reduce skewness and make distributions more Gaussian-like. The method operates on a frozen feature extractor after the first task and classifies based on the Mahalanobis distance to prototypes, without explicit training on new tasks.",
    "Experimental Setup": "Experiments were conducted on multiple publicly available datasets and scenarios. For **Many-Shot CIL (MSCIL)**: CIFAR100, TinyImageNet, and ImageNet-Subset, with various task splits (e.g., 50 initial classes + 5/10/20 incremental tasks). For **Few-Shot CIL (FSCIL)**: CIFAR100, miniImageNet, and Caltech-UCSD Birds-200-2011 (CUB200), with 60 base classes and 40 new classes (e.g., 8-step 5-way 5-shot incremental tasks). For **Pre-trained Models (Vision Transformers)**: Split-CIFAR100, Split-ImageNet-R, and CoRe50 (domain-incremental learning). The main network architecture used was ResNet-18, and for pretrained models, ViT-B/16 pretrained on ImageNet-21k. Validation was performed by comparing average incremental accuracy against numerous state-of-the-art exemplar-free and exemplar-based CIL methods, as well as a joint training upper bound.",
    "Limitations": "The proposed approach relies on a strong feature extractor or a substantial amount of data in the first task to learn high-quality initial representations, as it reuses these frozen features without updating them. Consequently, the method is not well-suited for training from scratch with small initial tasks. It also notes that the current theory would need to be extended to model feature distributions that undergo feature drift during continuous training, beyond just prototype drift, by accounting for covariance changes.",
    "Future Research Directions": "A primary future research direction is to adapt FeCAM to CIL settings where the feature representations are continually learned, rather than remaining static after the first task. This would involve extending the theoretical framework to account for and model covariance changes in feature distributions that experience drift during ongoing training.",
    "Experiment Code": "def shrink_cov(cov):    diag_mean = np.mean(np.diagonal(cov))    off_diag = np.copy(cov)    np.fill_diagonal(off_diag,0.0)    mask = off_diag != 0.0    off_diag_mean = (off_diag*mask).sum() / mask.sum()    iden = np.eye(cov.shape[0])    alpha1 = 1    alpha2  = 0    cov_ = cov + (alpha1*diag_mean*iden) + (alpha2*off_diag_mean*(1-iden))    return cov_\ndef normalize_cov(cov_mat):    norm_cov_mat = []    for cov in cov_mat:        sd = np.sqrt(np.diagonal(cov))  # standard deviations of the variables        cov = cov/(np.matmul(np.expand_dims(sd,1),np.expand_dims(sd,0)))        norm_cov_mat.append(cov)    return norm_cov_mat\ndef _mahalanobis(dist, cov=None):    if cov is None:        cov = np.eye(768)    inv_covmat = np.linalg.pinv(cov)  # pseudo-inverse of an invertible matrix is same as its inverse    left_term = np.matmul(dist, inv_covmat)    mahal = np.matmul(left_term, dist.T)     return np.diagonal(mahal, 0)\nclass_mean_set = []\naccuracy_history = []\ncov_mat = []\nshrink_cov_mat = []\n\nshrink = True\n\nfor task_id, train_dataset in enumerate(scenario_train):\n    train_loader = np.concatenate(y)\n\n    for i in range(task_id * 10, (task_id+1)*10):\n        image_class_mask = (y == i)\n        class_mean_set.append(np.mean(X[image_class_mask],axis=0))\n        cov = np.cov(X[image_class_mask].T)\n        cov_mat.append(cov)\n        if shrink:\n            shrink_cov_mat.append(shrink_cov(cov))\n    norm_cov_mat = normalize_cov(shrink_cov_mat)\n\n\n    test_ds =     "Experiment Result": "The experiments use a frozen `vit_base_patch16_224_in21k` model, pretrained on ImageNet-21k, as the feature extractor. Feature vectors are L2-normalized. Classification is based on the anisotropic Mahalanobis distance to class prototypes, which are computed as the mean of extracted features for each class. Per-class covariance matrices are computed, then subjected to shrinkage and correlation normalization.\n\n**Common Settings Across Datasets:**\n- **Feature Extractor**: `vit_base_patch16_224_in21k`, pretrained=True. The model is effectively frozen after the initial feature extraction (no explicit training loop beyond task 0 initialization in `models/fecam.py`, and direct scripts use pre-extracted features). Output feature dimension is 768.\n- **Feature Normalization**: L2 normalization (`F.normalize`) is applied to feature vectors before computing distances.\n- **Class Prototypes**: Computed as the mean of feature vectors for each class.\n- **Covariance Matrix Calculation**: Per-class covariance matrices are computed from the feature vectors.\n- **Covariance Shrinkage**: Applied using the `shrink_cov` function with specific `alpha1` and `alpha2` parameters (these vary by dataset).\n- **Correlation Normalization**: Applied using the `normalize_cov` function, making diagonal elements of the covariance matrix equal to 1.\n- **Mahalanobis Distance**: `np.linalg.pinv` (pseudo-inverse) is used for the inverse covariance matrix.\n- **Tukey's Ladder of Powers Transformation**: While mentioned in the method, it is not explicitly applied in the provided `FeCAM_vit_*.py` experimental scripts.\n\n**Dataset-Specific Settings:**\n1.  **CIFAR-100 (FeCAM_vit_cifar100.py)**:\n    - **Dataset**: CIFAR-100, class-incremental scenario.\n    - **Incremental Setup**: 10 initial classes, then 10 classes per increment, for a total of 10 tasks (100 classes).\n    - **Transformations**: `ToTensor()`, `Resize((224))`. A `ClassIncremental` scenario from `continuum` is used.\n    - **Batch Size**: 512.\n    - **Covariance Shrinkage Parameters**: `alpha1 = 1`, `alpha2 = 0`.\n\n2.  **Core50 (FeCAM_vit_core50.py)**:\n    - **Dataset**: Core50, using a custom `Core50Dataset` class.\n    - **Incremental Setup**: 8 tasks, 50 classes total.\n    - **Transformations**: `Resize(256)`, `CenterCrop(224)`, `ToTensor()`.\n    - **Batch Size**: 1024 for training class feature extraction, 512 for testing.\n    - **Covariance Shrinkage Parameters**: `alpha1 = 1`, `alpha2 = 0`.\n    - **Special Covariance Handling**: For tasks after the first (task_id > 0), the per-class `shrink_cov_mat` for class `i` is updated by averaging `(shrink_cov_mat[i] + current_task_cov_for_class_i) / 2`.\n\n3.  **Imagenet-r (FeCAM_vit_imagenet-r.py)**:\n    - **Dataset**: Imagenet-r, using a custom `Imagenet_r` dataset class.\n    - **Incremental Setup**: 10 tasks, 20 classes per task.\n    - **Transformations**: `transforms.Resize(256)`, `transforms.CenterCrop(224)`, `transforms.ToTensor()`.\n    - **Batch Size**: 1024.\n    - **Data Split**: For each task, the loaded dataset is split into 80% for training feature extraction (to compute means/covariances) and 20% for testing. The test dataset accumulates across tasks.\n    - **Covariance Shrinkage Parameters**: `alpha1 = 10`, `alpha2 = 10` (note: these are different from CIFAR-100 and Core50)."

    "Title": "Meta-Learning without Memorization",
    "Main Contributions": "The paper identifies and formalizes the \"memorization problem\" in meta-learning, where models overfit meta-training tasks by ignoring task-specific training data, particularly in non-mutually-exclusive task distributions. It proposes an information-theoretic meta-regularization (MR) objective to prioritize data-driven adaptation, enabling effective meta-learning without requiring mutually-exclusive tasks. The research demonstrates MR's applicability to gradient-based (MAML) and contextual (CNP) meta-learning, showing substantial performance gains, and provides a PAC-Bayes bound for theoretical justification.",
    "Methodology": "The core methodology introduces a meta-regularization (MR) objective based on information theory to encourage models to rely on task-specific training data (D) for predictions, rather than memorizing task information within meta-parameters (θ). Two specific approaches are proposed: 1) MR on activations, which introduces an intermediate stochastic bottleneck variable z* and minimizes DKL(q(z*|x*,θ)||r(z*)) alongside the training loss to restrict information flow. 2) MR on weights, which penalizes the information complexity of meta-parameters θ by adding DKL(q(θ; θµ,θσ)||r(θ)) to the objective, specifically applied to parameters not involved in task adaptation. This approach is theoretically supported by a PAC-Bayes generalization bound.",
    "Experimental Setup": "The study evaluated Model-Agnostic Meta-Learning (MAML) and Conditional Neural Processes (CNP) in non-mutually-exclusive settings, with the proposed meta-regularization applied to either activations (MR-A) or weights (MR-W). Experiments were conducted on: 1) a toy sinusoid regression problem with amplitude as an input feature, 2) a realistic pose prediction task utilizing Pascal 3D data (50 meta-training, 15 meta-testing objects), and adapted few-shot classification benchmarks including Omniglot (20-way 1/5-shot) and MiniImagenet (5-way 1/5-shot). Baselines included standard MAML/CNP, fine-tuning, nearest-neighbor, TAML, weight decay, and Bayes-by-Backprop (BbB). Performance was measured using Test Mean Squared Error (MSE) for regression tasks and Meta-test Accuracy for classification, with hyperparameters tuned via cross-validation. Pre-update accuracy was also analyzed to diagnose the memorization problem.",
    "Limitations": "The identified \"memorization problem\" itself represents a significant limitation of existing meta-learning algorithms, as it necessitates careful and often complex design of mutually-exclusive tasks. While the proposed MR-on-activations approach is generally effective, it can occasionally fail to prevent memorization, hypothesized to occur when the network manages to encode only minimal predictive information into the bottlenecked activations. Furthermore, the effectiveness of meta-regularization is sensitive to the tuning of its coefficient β; an excessively small β may still lead to memorization, while an overly large β can hinder the model's ability to store necessary information in its weights for adaptation.",
    "Future Research Directions": "The authors suggest exploring the prevalence and applicability of the memorization problem in other conditional prediction problems beyond the scope of meta-learning. They also point to real-world application domains where the issue is likely to be significant, such as personalized speech recognition systems, learning robots that adapt to dynamic environments, and learning goal-conditioned manipulation skills using trial-and-error data. The overarching future goal is to enhance the general applicability and effectiveness of meta-learning algorithms across diverse problem domains.",
